Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because my eating has gotten really inconsistent. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets. It’s been all over the place, and it started to worry me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure exactly how much my weight has changed, but my appetite has definitely been up and down. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen late eating crackers or sweets. It’s inconsistent enough that it started worrying me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not so much gaining weight specifically. It’s more that my eating feels out of control lately—some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets. That’s what started worrying me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, feeling like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes. Not every day, but there are nights when everyone’s gone to bed and I’m standing in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets, and it feels like I just keep going even though I didn’t really plan to. Other weeks I barely touch dinner, so it’s been really all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to get rid of the calories from the food you've eaten, like by vomiting or using laxatives?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t do that. I haven’t made myself throw up or used laxatives to try to get rid of food.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after eating those crackers or sweets late at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do feel guilty afterward. Not just about the food, really. I can get stuck for hours replaying small things, like if I bought an expensive tube of pigment or snapped at my spouse, and then I feel like I’ve somehow messed up the whole household because of it.\n\nDoctor: Is the guilt you feel after eating late at night specifically about eating, or is it part of a larger pattern of feeling guilty about various things?\n\nPatient: It’s more part of a bigger pattern. The eating can be one thing I feel bad about, but I’ll also spend hours going over little things—like buying art supplies that cost too much, or snapping at my spouse—and then it feels like I’ve ruined everything at home.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would go back to normal if you weren't feeling so guilty or anxious about other things in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I think the guilt and anxiety make it worse, but the eating itself has really been all over the place. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets. That’s part of what finally made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the eating issues would be your main concern if you weren't feeling guilty or anxious about other things in your life?\n\nPatient: I think it would still be a concern, yes. The guilt makes it feel worse, but what really pushed me to come in is that my eating has been so unpredictable—some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: Is the unpredictable eating pattern what's primarily bothering you, or is it the guilt and anxiety you feel about other things that's more troubling?\n\nPatient: It’s more the eating pattern itself that’s bothering me. The guilt and anxiety are there, but what really made me feel like I needed to come in was how all over the place it’s been—some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m up after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have nights where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like those times you described eating crackers or sweets after everyone's gone to bed?\n\nPatient: It’s not every night, but it happens enough that it worries me. I’ll have stretches where I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets. I guess that back-and-forth is what feels so out of control.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you typically have nights where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: It kind of comes and goes. I wouldn’t say it’s every week the same, but on the worse weeks maybe a couple nights I’ll end up in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets. Other weeks I’m barely touching dinner, so it’s hard to give an exact number.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many nights a week do you find yourself eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: On average, maybe a couple nights a week when it’s a bad stretch. It’s not steady every week, though. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time at least once a week?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think at least once a week during the stretches when my eating is all over the place. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, but then other nights I’m in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets after everyone’s gone to bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have control over what you eat during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Once I’m standing there eating crackers or sweets, it feels like I just keep going even though I didn’t really plan to. It’s like my eating is all over the place—some weeks I barely want dinner, and then other nights I can’t seem to stop once I start.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. [
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.4, F50.5\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because my eating has gotten really inconsistent. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets. It’s been all over the place, and it started to worry me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure exactly how much my weight has changed, but my appetite has definitely been up and down. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen late eating crackers or sweets. It’s inconsistent enough that it started worrying me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not so much gaining weight specifically. It’s more that my eating feels out of control lately—some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets. That’s what started worrying me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, feeling like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes. Not every day, but there are nights when everyone’s gone to bed and I’m standing in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets, and it feels like I just keep going even though I didn’t really plan to. Other weeks I barely touch dinner, so it’s been really all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to get rid of the calories from the food you've eaten, like by vomiting or using laxatives?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t do that. I haven’t made myself throw up or used laxatives to try to get rid of food.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after eating those crackers or sweets late at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do feel guilty afterward. Not just about the food, really. I can get stuck for hours replaying small things, like if I bought an expensive tube of pigment or snapped at my spouse, and then I feel like I’ve somehow messed up the whole household because of it.\n\nDoctor: Is the guilt you feel after eating late at night specifically about eating, or is it part of a larger pattern of feeling guilty about various things?\n\nPatient: It’s more part of a bigger pattern. The eating can be one thing I feel bad about, but I’ll also spend hours going over little things—like buying art supplies that cost too much, or snapping at my spouse—and then it feels like I’ve ruined everything at home.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would go back to normal if you weren't feeling so guilty or anxious about other things in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I think the guilt and anxiety make it worse, but the eating itself has really been all over the place. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets. That’s part of what finally made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the eating issues would be your main concern if you weren't feeling guilty or anxious about other things in your life?\n\nPatient: I think it would still be a concern, yes. The guilt makes it feel worse, but what really pushed me to come in is that my eating has been so unpredictable—some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: Is the unpredictable eating pattern what's primarily bothering you, or is it the guilt and anxiety you feel about other things that's more troubling?\n\nPatient: It’s more the eating pattern itself that’s bothering me. The guilt and anxiety are there, but what really made me feel like I needed to come in was how all over the place it’s been—some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m up after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have nights where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like those times you described eating crackers or sweets after everyone's gone to bed?\n\nPatient: It’s not every night, but it happens enough that it worries me. I’ll have stretches where I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets. I guess that back-and-forth is what feels so out of control.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you typically have nights where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: It kind of comes and goes. I wouldn’t say it’s every week the same, but on the worse weeks maybe a couple nights I’ll end up in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets. Other weeks I’m barely touching dinner, so it’s hard to give an exact number.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many nights a week do you find yourself eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: On average, maybe a couple nights a week when it’s a bad stretch. It’s not steady every week, though. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time at least once a week?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think at least once a week during the stretches when my eating is all over the place. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, but then other nights I’m in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets after everyone’s gone to bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have control over what you eat during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Once I’m standing there eating crackers or sweets, it feels like I just keep going even though I didn’t really plan to. It’s like my eating is all over the place—some weeks I barely want dinner, and then other nights I can’t seem to stop once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time more than once a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes it’s more than once a week, especially when my eating is really off. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then I’ll have a few nights where I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I think the guilt and anxiety make it worse, but the eating itself has really been all over the place. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets. That’s part of what finally made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the eating issues would be your main concern if you weren't feeling guilty or anxious about other things in your life?\n\nPatient: I think it would still be a concern, yes. The guilt makes it feel worse, but what really pushed me to come in is that my eating has been so unpredictable—some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: Is the unpredictable eating pattern what's primarily bothering you, or is it the guilt and anxiety you feel about other things that's more troubling?\n\nPatient: It’s more the eating pattern itself that’s bothering me. The guilt and anxiety are there, but what really made me feel like I needed to come in was how all over the place it’s been—some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m up after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have nights where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like those times you described eating crackers or sweets after everyone's gone to bed?\n\nPatient: It’s not every night, but it happens enough that it worries me. I’ll have stretches where I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets. I guess that back-and-forth is what feels so out of control.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you typically have nights where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: It kind of comes and goes. I wouldn’t say it’s every week the same, but on the worse weeks maybe a couple nights I’ll end up in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets. Other weeks I’m barely touching dinner, so it’s hard to give an exact number.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many nights a week do you find yourself eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: On average, maybe a couple nights a week when it’s a bad stretch. It’s not steady every week, though. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time at least once a week?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think at least once a week during the stretches when my eating is all over the place. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, but then other nights I’m in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets after everyone’s gone to bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have control over what you eat during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. No, I don’t think it’s that frequent. It’s more like some weeks my eating is really off, and on a few nights I’ll end up in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets after everyone’s gone to bed. The eating can be one thing I feel bad about, but I’ll also spend hours going over little things—like buying art supplies that cost too much, or snapping at my spouse—and then it feels like I’ve ruined everything at home.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would go back to normal if you weren't feeling so guilty or anxious about other things in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I think the guilt and anxiety make it worse, but the eating itself has really been all over the place. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets. That’s part of what finally made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the eating issues would be your main concern if you weren't feeling guilty or anxious about other things in your life?\n\nPatient: I think it would still be a concern, yes. The guilt makes it feel worse, but what really pushed me to come in is that my eating has been so unpredictable—some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: Is the unpredictable eating pattern what's primarily bothering you, or is it the guilt and anxiety you feel about other things that's more troubling?\n\nPatient: It’s more the eating pattern itself that’s bothering me. The guilt and anxiety are there, but what really made me feel like I needed to come in was how all over the place it’s been—some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m up after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have nights where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like those times you described eating crackers or sweets after everyone's gone to bed?\n\nPatient: It’s not every night, but it happens enough that it worries me. I’ll have stretches where I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets. I guess that back-and-forth is what feels so out of control.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you typically have nights where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: It kind of comes and goes. I wouldn’t say it’s every week the same, but on the worse weeks maybe a couple nights I’ll end up in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets. Other weeks I’m barely touching dinner, so it’s hard to give an exact number.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many nights a week do you find yourself eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: On average, maybe a couple nights a week when it’s a bad stretch. It’s not steady every week, though. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time at least once a week?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think at least once a week during the stretches when my eating is all over the place. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, but then other nights I’m in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets after everyone’s gone to bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have control over what you eat during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Once I’m standing there eating crackers or sweets, it feels like I just keep going even though I didn’t really plan to. It’s like my eating is all over the place—some weeks I barely want dinner, and then other nights I can’t seem to stop once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time more than once a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes it’s more than once a week, especially when my eating is really off. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then I’ll have a few nights where I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time almost every day?\n\nPatient: Almost every day? Other times I barely touch dinner.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, during those nights it does feel hard to stop once I start. I’ll go in thinking I’m just having a few crackers or something sweet, and then I’m still standing there eating after everyone’s gone to bed. I haven’t made myself throw up or used laxatives to try to get rid of food.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after eating those crackers or sweets late at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do feel guilty afterward. Not just about the food, really. I can get stuck for hours replaying small things, like if I bought an expensive tube of pigment or snapped at my spouse, and then I feel like I’ve somehow messed up the whole household because of it.\n\nDoctor: Is the guilt you feel after eating late at night specifically about eating, or is it part of a larger pattern of feeling guilty about various things?\n\nPatient: It’s more part of a bigger pattern. The eating can be one thing I feel bad about, but I’ll also spend hours going over little things—like buying art supplies that cost too much, or snapping at my spouse—and then it feels like I’ve ruined everything at home.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would go back to normal if you weren't feeling so guilty or anxious about other things in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I think the guilt and anxiety make it worse, but the eating itself has really been all over the place. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets. That’s part of what finally made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the eating issues would be your main concern if you weren't feeling guilty or anxious about other things in your life?\n\nPatient: I think it would still be a concern, yes. The guilt makes it feel worse, but what really pushed me to come in is that my eating has been so unpredictable—some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: Is the unpredictable eating pattern what's primarily bothering you, or is it the guilt and anxiety you feel about other things that's more troubling?\n\nPatient: It’s more the eating pattern itself that’s bothering me. The guilt and anxiety are there, but what really made me feel like I needed to come in was how all over the place it’s been—some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m up after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have nights where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like those times you described eating crackers or sweets after everyone's gone to bed?\n\nPatient: It’s not every night, but it happens enough that it worries me. I’ll have stretches where I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets. I guess that back-and-forth is what feels so out of control.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you typically have nights where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: It kind of comes and goes. I wouldn’t say it’s every week the same, but on the worse weeks maybe a couple nights I’ll end up in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets. Other weeks I’m barely touching dinner, so it’s hard to give an exact number.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many nights a week do you find yourself eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: On average, maybe a couple nights a week when it’s a bad stretch. It’s not steady every week, though. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time at least once a week?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think at least once a week during the stretches when my eating is all over the place. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, but then other nights I’m in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets after everyone’s gone to bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have control over what you eat during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Once I’m standing there eating crackers or sweets, it feels like I just keep going even though I didn’t really plan to. It’s like my eating is all over the place—some weeks I barely want dinner, and then other nights I can’t seem to stop once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time more than once a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes it’s more than once a week, especially when my eating is really off. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then I’ll have a few nights where I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time almost every day?\n\nPatient: Almost every day? No, I don’t think it’s that frequent. It’s more like some weeks my eating is really off, and on a few nights I’ll end up in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets after everyone’s gone to bed. Other times I barely touch dinner.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, during those nights it does feel hard to stop once I start. I’ll go in thinking I’m just having a few crackers or something sweet, and then I’m still standing there eating after everyone’s gone to bed. It’s part of what made me realize my eating’s been really all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control how much you eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I don’t feel very in control of it on those nights. I’ll think I’m just going to have a little bit, like crackers or something sweet, and then I keep eating more than I meant to after everyone’s gone to bed. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.4, F50.5\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because my eating has gotten really inconsistent. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets. It’s been all over the place, and it started to worry me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure exactly how much my weight has changed, but my appetite has definitely been up and down. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen late eating crackers or sweets. It’s inconsistent enough that it started worrying me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not so much gaining weight specifically. It’s more that my eating feels out of control lately—some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets. That’s what started worrying me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, feeling like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes. Not every day, but there are nights when everyone’s gone to bed and I’m standing in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets, and it feels like I just keep going even though I didn’t really plan to. Other weeks I barely touch dinner, so it’s been really all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever try to get rid of the calories from the food you've eaten, like by vomiting or using laxatives?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t do that. I haven’t made myself throw up or used laxatives to try to get rid of food.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after eating those crackers or sweets late at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do feel guilty afterward. Not just about the food, really. I can get stuck for hours replaying small things, like if I bought an expensive tube of pigment or snapped at my spouse, and then I feel like I’ve somehow messed up the whole household because of it.\n\nDoctor: Is the guilt you feel after eating late at night specifically about eating, or is it part of a larger pattern of feeling guilty about various things?\n\nPatient: It’s more part of a bigger pattern. The eating can be one thing I feel bad about, but I’ll also spend hours going over little things—like buying art supplies that cost too much, or snapping at my spouse—and then it feels like I’ve ruined everything at home.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits would go back to normal if you weren't feeling so guilty or anxious about other things in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I think the guilt and anxiety make it worse, but the eating itself has really been all over the place. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets. That’s part of what finally made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the eating issues would be your main concern if you weren't feeling guilty or anxious about other things in your life?\n\nPatient: I think it would still be a concern, yes. The guilt makes it feel worse, but what really pushed me to come in is that my eating has been so unpredictable—some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: Is the unpredictable eating pattern what's primarily bothering you, or is it the guilt and anxiety you feel about other things that's more troubling?\n\nPatient: It’s more the eating pattern itself that’s bothering me. The guilt and anxiety are there, but what really made me feel like I needed to come in was how all over the place it’s been—some weeks I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m up after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: How often do you have nights where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time, like those times you described eating crackers or sweets after everyone's gone to bed?\n\nPatient: It’s not every night, but it happens enough that it worries me. I’ll have stretches where I barely eat dinner, and then other nights I’m standing in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets. I guess that back-and-forth is what feels so out of control.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you typically have nights where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: It kind of comes and goes. I wouldn’t say it’s every week the same, but on the worse weeks maybe a couple nights I’ll end up in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets. Other weeks I’m barely touching dinner, so it’s hard to give an exact number.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many nights a week do you find yourself eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: On average, maybe a couple nights a week when it’s a bad stretch. It’s not steady every week, though. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s gone to bed eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time at least once a week?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think at least once a week during the stretches when my eating is all over the place. Some weeks I barely eat dinner, but then other nights I’m in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets after everyone’s gone to bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have control over what you eat during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Once I’m standing there eating crackers or sweets, it feels like I just keep going even though I didn’t really plan to. It’s like my eating is all over the place—some weeks I barely want dinner, and then other nights I can’t seem to stop once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time more than once a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes it’s more than once a week, especially when my eating is really off. Some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then I’ll have a few nights where I’m in the kitchen after everyone’s asleep eating crackers or sweets.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time almost every day?\n\nPatient: Almost every day? No, I don’t think it’s that frequent. It’s more like some weeks my eating is really off, and on a few nights I’ll end up in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets after everyone’s gone to bed. Other times I barely touch dinner.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, during those nights it does feel hard to stop once I start. I’ll go in thinking I’m just having a few crackers or something sweet, and then I’m still standing there eating after everyone’s gone to bed. It’s part of what made me realize my eating’s been really all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to control how much you eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I don’t feel very in control of it on those nights. I’ll think I’m just going to have a little bit, like crackers or something sweet, and then I keep eating more than I meant to after everyone’s gone to bed. It’s not every night, but when it happens, it feels like my eating is just really out of whack.\n\nDoctor: Do you have episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time at least three times a week?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s as specific as three times a week. It’s more that my eating has been all over the place—some weeks I barely touch dinner, and then other nights I’m in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets after everyone’s gone to bed.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
